Films with higher impurity density show accelerated early degradation, establishing this parameter as an early-warning metric for stability screening. Extending this framework to degradation tracking further reveals coupled photo- and thermo-chemical contributions to impurity-mediated instability. Overall, this work establishes a practical chemically-validated diagnostic imaging framework for rapid pre-screening of perovskite films to enable stable and scalable PSCs.

</details>


### [50] [Ultra-high vacuum Raman platform for in situ characterization of graphene nanoribbons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35556)
*Jeong Ha Hwang,Amogh Kinikar,Lukas Rotach,Andres Ortega-Guerrero,Carlo A. Pignedoli,Klaus Muellen,Thorsten G. Englmann,Xinliang Feng,Pascal Ruffieux,Roman Fasel,Mickael L. Perrin,Gabriela Borin Barin*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: A home-built UHV Raman vacuum suitcase (RVS) with temperature control and gas dosing enables in situ, air-free Raman characterization of air-sensitive graphene nanoribbons, tracking their on-surface synthesis, thermal evolution, and oxidation behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Atomically precise GNRs have tunable electronic and magnetic properties useful for electronics and spintronics, but their unpaired pi-electrons make them highly air-sensitive. Standard characterization after air exposure probes oxidized species rather than the pristine material, so there is a central need to access intrinsic vibrational and electronic signatures without breaking vacuum.

Method: Developed a home-built ultra-high vacuum (UHV) Raman platform—a portable Raman vacuum suitcase (RVS) integrating temperature control and precise gas dosing—that preserves sample integrity by preventing air exposure and permits in situ monitoring of growth kinetics, lattice dynamics, and reactive-edge responses.

Result: Using the platform, the authors monitored on-surface synthesis of 7- and 9-atom-wide armchair GNRs (7- and 9-AGNRs), quantified the evolution of 7-AGNR Raman modes across 162-748 K, and resolved chemical changes upon controlled O2 exposure consistent with oxidation at reactive zigzag sites.

Conclusion: UHV Raman spectroscopy with the RVS provides a route to accessing intrinsic vibrational signatures of low-dimensional quantum materials under controlled environments.

Abstract: Atomically precise graphene nanoribbons (GNRs) exhibit tunable electronic and magnetic properties governed by edge topology and finite-size effects, which make them versatile platforms for next-generation electronic and spintronic applications. However, the unpaired pi-electrons responsible for their magnetic properties simultaneously make them highly susceptible to chemical degradation under ambient conditions. This intrinsic reactivity poses a central experimental challenge: accessing vibrational and electronic signatures of air-sensitive GNRs during synthesis and under controlled environments without breaking vacuum. Once the material has been exposed to air, standard characterization techniques would probe oxidized or chemically modified species rather than the pristine form. Here, we overcome this limitation by developing a home-built ultra-high vacuum (UHV) Raman platform designed to preserve sample integrity by preventing air exposure and to enable in situ investigation of material properties. The portable Raman vacuum suitcase (RVS) integrates temperature control and precise gas dosing, allowing direct monitoring of growth kinetics, lattice dynamics, and reactive-edge responses under well-defined thermal and chemical environments. Using this platform, we monitor the on-surface synthesis of 7- and 9-atom-wide armchair GNRs (7- and 9-AGNRs), quantify the evolution of 7-AGNR Raman modes over a wide temperature range (162-748 K), and resolve chemical changes upon controlled O2 exposure that are consistent with oxidation at the reactive zigzag sites. These results establish UHV Raman spectroscopy with the RVS as a route to accessing the intrinsic vibrational signatures of low-dimensional quantum materials under controlled environments.

</details>


### [51] [Pyroelectric thermo-wave probing of BaZr0.2Ti0.8O3 ceramics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35667)
*Nicholas V. Morozovsky,Yuriy O. Zagorodniy,Mykola Yelisieiev,Oleksandr S. Pylypchuk,Mykola O. Semenenko,Iryna V. Kondakova,Lesya P. Yurchenko,Viktor Bovtun,Valentin V. Laguta,Eugene A. Eliseev,Volodymyr O. Dzhagan,Anna N. Morozovska*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Pyroelectric thermo-wave probing of BaZr0.2Ti0.8O3 ceramics reveals a pronounced, surface-asymmetric pyroelectric response that depends strongly on electric-field pre-history, indicating non-ergodic relaxor-type polar states and coexistence of ordered ferroelectric and relaxor phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: BaxZr1-xTiO3 ceramics (0.15 < x < 0.25) are promising for tunable capacitors, multilayered ceramic capacitors, and piezoelectric actuators due to high permittivity, small leakage currents, and low dissipation factor, and they show relaxor properties from local chemical strains caused by isovalent Zr4+ substitution of Ti4+. However, the pyroelectric properties of compositions near x~0.2 had not been studied, leaving a knowledge gap that this work aims to fill.

Method: The authors performed pyroelectric thermo-wave probing of BaZr0.2Ti0.8O3 ceramics prepared by solid-state synthesis, examining polarized, depolarized, relaxed, and restored polarized states. Complementary characterization included room-temperature X-ray diffraction, temperature-dependent Raman spectroscopy, and dielectric permittivity measurements analyzed with a modified Curie-Weiss law.

Result: Thermo-wave probing revealed a pronounced pyroelectric response that can be strongly asymmetric between opposite ceramic surfaces, with asymmetry and response profiles depending significantly on electric-field cycling pre-history. XRD showed virtual absence of macroscopic tetragonality and weak asymmetry of the (200) peak. The Raman band at 714 cm-1 decreased in relative intensity upon heating, evidencing a diffuse ferroelectric-paraelectric transition between 30 C and 40 C. Dielectric permittivity followed a modified Curie-Weiss law with exponent 1.4.

Conclusion: The pronounced, pre-history-dependent, surface-asymmetric pyroelectric response points to non-ergodic relaxor-type polar states in BaZr0.2Ti0.8O3 ceramics. Small tetragonality within Ti-enriched polar nanoregions, a diffuse phase transition, and a Curie-Weiss exponent of 1.4 together indicate coexistence of ordered ferroelectric and relaxor states in the material.

Abstract: BaxZr1-xTiO3 ceramics with 0.15 < x < 0.25, which have high dielectric permittivity, small leakage currents and low dissipation factor, are promising materials for tunable capacitor devices, multilayered ceramic capacitors and piezoelectric actuators. These materials reveal relaxor properties due to the local chemical strains caused by the isovalent substitution of Ti4+ ions by Zr4+ ions with a larger ionic size. However, to the best of our knowledge, the pyroelectric properties of BaxZr1-xTiO3 ceramics with x~0.2 have not been studied. To fill the gap in knowledge, we perform the pyroelectric thermo-wave probing of BaZr0.2Ti0.8O3 ceramics prepared by the solid-state synthesis. Results of the thermo-wave probing of the most interesting polar states, namely polarized, depolarized, relaxed and restored polarized states, reveal the pronounced pyroelectric response that can be strongly asymmetric with respect to the opposite surfaces of the ceramics. The asymmetry and profiles of pyroelectric response depend significantly on the pre-history of the electric field cycling indicating possible non-ergodic relaxor-type polar states in the ceramic sample. X-ray diffraction spectrum, recorded at room temperature, reveals the virtual absence of the macroscopic tetragonality and the weak asymmetry of the (200) peak, which indicate the small tetragonality inside the Ti-enriched polar nanoregions. Decrease of the relative intensity of the Raman band at 714 cm-1 occurring upon heating evidences the diffuse ferroelectric-paraelectric phase transition between 30 C and 40 C. The temperature dependence of the dielectric permittivity obeys modified Curie-Weiss law with power 1.4, indicating that both ordered ferroelectric and relaxor states coexist in the BaTi0.8Zr0.2O3 ceramics.

</details>


### [52] [A Si-on-SiC Platform for Interfacing with Vanadium Dopants in the Telecom O-Band](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35702)
*Timothy Draher,Nolan Bitner,Vasileios Niaouris,Claire E. McDermott,David Czaplewski,Supratik Guha,David D. Awschalom,F. Joseph Heremans,Alan M. Dibos*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper introduces a silicon-on-SiC heterogeneous photonic platform where silicon nanocavities evanescently couple to shallow vanadium color centers in 4H-SiC, enabling Purcell-enhanced, telecom O-band single-photon emission with efficient ZPL collection and charge-state repumping through a single lensed fiber.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vanadium color centers in SiC are promising for quantum network nodes due to telecom O-band emission, sub-microsecond optical lifetimes, and second-scale spin relaxation times. However, efficient photon extraction from the high-refractive-index SiC host is challenging, limiting practical spin-photon interfaces.

Method: A silicon-on-SiC heterogeneous photonic platform is developed in which silicon nanocavities evanescently couple to shallow vanadium dopants in commercial 4H-SiC. The approach enables efficient zero-phonon line (ZPL) collection under resonant excitation. Measurements are performed on both as-grown ensembles and dilute implanted samples, with the full optical interface driven through a single lensed fiber, including a 905 nm repump laser.

Result: In as-grown ensembles, Purcell-enhanced photoluminescence with transient spectral hole burning linewidths of 50 MHz on microsecond timescales is observed. In dilute implanted samples, individual vanadium centers are isolated with high-purity single-photon emission and a Purcell-enhanced lifetime of 109 ns. The 905 nm repump laser recovers the vanadium charge state with 95% efficiency.

Conclusion: Si-on-SiC is established as a scalable, foundry-compatible platform for telecom-wavelength spin-photon interfaces, combining Purcell-enhanced single-photon emission from vanadium centers with efficient charge-state control via a single-fiber optical interface.

Abstract: Vanadium color centers in silicon carbide (SiC) offer telecom O-band emission, sub-microsecond optical lifetimes, and second-scale spin relaxation times, rendering them promising candidates for quantum network nodes. However, efficient photon extraction from the high-refractive-index SiC host remains challenging. Here, we introduce a silicon-on-SiC heterogeneous photonic platform in which silicon nanocavities evanescently couple to shallow vanadium dopants in commercial 4H-SiC, enabling efficient zero-phonon line (ZPL) collection under resonant excitation. In as-grown ensembles, we observe Purcell-enhanced photoluminescence with transient spectral hole burning linewidths of 50 MHz on microsecond timescales. In dilute implanted samples, we isolate individual vanadium centers with high-purity single photon emission and a Purcell-enhanced lifetime of 109 ns. The entire optical interface operates through a single lensed fiber, including a 905 nm repump laser that recovers the vanadium charge state with 95% efficiency. These results establish Si-on-SiC as a scalable, foundry-compatible platform for telecom-wavelength spin-photon interfaces.

</details>


### [53] [Catalogue of Collinear Magnetic Structures in Stoichiometric Materials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35712)
*Liangliang Huang,Houhao Wang,Yuanze Song,Ruixi Pu,Xiangang Wan,Feng Tang*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Using spin space groups and maximal collinear subgroup enumeration, the authors generate 365,639 symmetry-allowed collinear magnetic structures across materials in the ICSD, determine ground-state symmetries for 7,824 magnets via first-principles calculations, and topologically classify them, yielding thousands of new magnetic topological materials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reliable determination of magnetic structures is essential for understanding emergent quantum properties of magnetic materials, but it typically relies on experiments such as neutron scattering. There is a need for a computational approach that presets and predicts candidate collinear magnetic structures to guide experimental characterization and discovery.

Method: Observing that about half of MAGNDATA's magnetic structures are collinear, the authors use spin space groups (SSGs) to preset collinear candidate structures from nonmagnetic crystal structures. Leveraging the fact that over 80% of collinear MAGNDATA magnets correspond to maximal subgroups of nonmagnetic groups (collinear SSGs), they enumerate all collinear magnetic structures formed at Wyckoff positions from maximal collinear SSG subgroups of the 230 nonmagnetic space groups. They then determine ground-state SSG symmetries of selected materials via first-principles calculations, followed by topological classification combining SSGs and magnetic space groups.

Result: Enumeration over 72,075 nonmagnetic stoichiometric structures from the ICSD yields 365,639 collinear magnetic structures. First-principles calculations determine ground-state SSG symmetries for 7,824 collinear magnets among 21,315 selected materials. The topological classification outputs thousands of new magnetic topological materials.

Conclusion: The experimentally known magnetic structures in MAGNDATA represent only a fraction of this computational collinear magnet database, which is expected to guide experimental magnetic structure characterization and stimulate further research toward spintronics applications.

Abstract: Magnetic materials persistently serve as a fertile platform for exploring novel quantum emergent phenom ena and advancing information technologies. Reliably determining their magnetic structures (MSs), which is essential for understanding their emergent quantum properties, usually depends on experiments such as neutron scattering. Here, noting that around half of the MSs in MAGNDATA (a database of experimentally determined MSs) are collinear, we adopt the spin space groups (SSGs) to preset the candidate MSs for a given nonmagnetic crystal structure to be collinear from the outset. On the other hand, more than 80% of the collinear magnets in MAGNDATA are subject to maximal subgroups of nonmagnetic groups which are collinear SSGs, and we then enumerate all collinear MSs formed at Wyckoff positions by the maximal collinear SSG subgroups of the 230 nonmagnetic space groups. With such exhaustive enumeration, we categorize all symmetry-allowed collinear MSs for the 72,075 nonmagnetic stoichiometric structures in Inorganic Crystal Structure Database, obtaining 365,639 collinear MSs. We then determine the ground-state SSG symmetries of 7,824 collinear magnets among the 21,315 selected materials using first-principles calculations. These magnets then undergo a comprehensive topological classification combining SSGs and magnetic space groups, outputting thousands of new magnetic topological materials. The experimentally determined MSs in MAGNDATA represent the tip of the iceberg in our computational database of collinear magnets, expected to guide MS characterization by experimentalists and further investigation towards spintronics applications.

</details>

### [54] [Growth and Interface Engineering of Superconducting TiN on Sapphire by Thermal-Laser Epitaxy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31721)
*Anthony Hyatt,Anand Ithepalli,Eegene Clara Chung,Yorick A. Birkholzer,Brendan Faeth,Huili Grace Xing,David A. Muller,Darrell G. Schlom,Debdeep Jena*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Thermal-laser epitaxy (TLE) of TiN on sapphire improves transport properties up to 1150 °C, but high ammonia pressure and temperature cause interfacial voids; an 850 °C TiN seed layer mitigates this, yielding record-low resistivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: TLE expands accessible pressure, temperature, and growth-rate regimes over conventional MBE, enabling in situ laser annealing, high-purity buffer layers, and efficient evaporation of refractory elements, making it attractive for low-loss superconducting resonators and Josephson-junction heterostructures. The study aims to exploit TLE for high-quality TiN on sapphire, a key material for such devices.

Method: TiN films were grown on sapphire by TLE, with growth temperature varied up to 1150 °C and a high ammonia overpressure. Interfacial degradation was addressed by introducing an initial TiN seed layer grown at 850 °C prior to the high-temperature main layer.

Result: Transport properties improved with increasing growth temperature up to 1150 °C, but the high ammonia pressure and elevated substrate temperature promoted reactions at the sapphire surface, creating voids at the TiN-substrate interface that increase interfacial surface area, introduce dangling bonds, and threaten tunnel-barrier heterostructures. With the 850 °C seed layer, the film exhibited a superconducting transition temperature of 5.8 K, a residual resistivity ratio of 12.2, and a resistivity of 1.19 µΩ·cm at 10 K — the lowest reported for TiN grown on sapphire.

Conclusion: An 850 °C TiN seed layer successfully mitigates sapphire/TiN interfacial void formation caused by the aggressive TLE growth conditions, enabling record-low-resistivity, high-quality TiN on sapphire suitable for superconducting resonator and Josephson-junction applications.

Abstract: Thermal-laser epitaxy (TLE) extends the accessible pressure, temperature, and growth-rate regimes of conventional molecular-beam epitaxy, enabling in situ laser annealing, high-purity buffer-layer growth, and efficient evaporation of refractory elements. These capabilities make TLE a promising platform for engineering low-loss superconducting resonators and Josephson-junction heterostructures. Here, we study the TLE growth of TiN on sapphire and observe improved transport properties with increasing growth temperature up to 1150 C. Unfortunately, we observe that the high ammonia pressure and elevated substrate temperatures required for optimal TiN properties promote reactions at the sapphire surface, resulting in voids at the TiN-substrate interface. These defects increase interfacial surface area, introduce dangling bonds, and could compromise tunnel-barrier heterostructures. We mitigate this degradation using an initial TiN seed layer grown at 850 C. With the seed layer, we achieve a superconducting transition temperature of 5.8 K, a residual resistivity ratio of 12.2, and a resistivity of 1.19 micro-ohm cm at 10 K, which, to our knowledge, is the lowest reported for TiN grown on sapphire.

</details>


### [55] [Quantum Geometric Origin of Yu-Shiba-Rusinov States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31920)
*Rui-Xing Zhang*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This work identifies a quantum geometric origin for the Yu-Shiba-Rusinov (YSR) bound-state structure in s-wave superconductors, where the band-projected quantum geometric quantity controls the number, energies, and symmetry of YSR states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classic YSR problem of magnetic impurities in s-wave superconductors is revisited to find a deeper origin for the bound-state structure beyond conventional descriptions.

Method: The authors introduce a quantum geometric quantity: the momentum-space average of the Bloch band projector over low-energy electrons, whose matrix rank, positive eigenvalues, and eigenvectors control the YSR states. They apply the theory to monolayer 1H-NbSe2.

Result: A superconductor with nontrivial normal-state quantum geometry can host more bound states than a trivial counterpart with the same electronic dispersion. For monolayer 1H-NbSe2, the geometric texture enforces three symmetry-distinct YSR channels for a magnetic point impurity at Nb or Se sites.

Conclusion: Normal-state wavefunction geometry can be probed via local YSR spectroscopy, opening a new route to studying quantum geometry in superconductors.

Abstract: In this work, we revisit the classic Yu-Shiba-Rusinov (YSR) problem of magnetic impurities in $s$-wave superconductors and uncover a quantum geometric origin of the YSR bound-state structure. The key geometric quantity is the momentum-space average of Bloch band projector over low-energy electrons, whose matrix rank, positive eigenvalues, and corresponding eigenvectors directly control the number, energies, and symmetry representations of the YSR states, respectively. As a result, a superconductor with nontrivial normal-state quantum geometry can host more bound states than its trivial counterpart, even if they share the same electronic dispersion. We apply our theory to monolayer $1H$-NbSe$_2$ and find that the geometric texture of its single superconducting band enforces three symmetry-distinct YSR channels for a magnetic point impurity at Nb or Se sites. Our work opens a route to probing normal-state wavefunction geometry through local YSR spectroscopy.

</details>


### [56] [Spin pumping reveals vortex-mediated angular-momentum dissipation at the superconducting transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32947)
*Maximilian Mangold,Alex Burg,Franz Weidenhiller,Thomas N. G. Meier,Hiroto Adachi,Lin Chen,Christian Back*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Spin pumping into a superconductor is normally expected to weaken below Tc, yet in Py/Nb heterostructures the induced magnetic damping is enhanced in a narrow window just below the superconducting transition, an effect tied to mobile Abrikosov vortices rather than quasiparticles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional wisdom holds that spin pumping into superconductors becomes less efficient below Tc because quasiparticle transport freezes out, leaving no known channel for the anomalous dissipation observed here. The authors aim to identify what mechanism can enhance rather than suppress magnetic damping in the superconducting state.

Method: Ferromagnetic resonance measurements on Py/Nb heterostructures, tracking spin-pumping-induced magnetic damping as a function of temperature across the superconducting transition and correlating it with the resistive state of the Nb layer.

Result: The damping exceeds its normal-state value in a narrow temperature interval immediately below Tc. The enhancement coincides with the finite-resistance vortex-creep regime and vanishes upon cooling into the pinned vortex state.

Conclusion: Mobile Abrikosov vortices supply an additional channel for spin-angular-momentum dissipation, so ferromagnetic resonance serves as a sensitive probe of dissipative vortex dynamics in superconductors. The findings overturn the quasiparticle-only picture of spin transport in superconductors.

Abstract: Spin pumping into superconductors is generally expected to become less efficient below the critical temperature $T_\textrm{c}$ because quasiparticle transport freezes out. Here we report a pronounced enhancement of the spin pumping-induced magnetic damping in Py/Nb heterostructures, confined to a narrow temperature interval immediately below the superconducting transition. The damping exceeds its normal-state value and therefore cannot be explained within the conventional quasiparticle picture of spin transport. The enhancement coincides with the finite-resistance vortex-creep regime of the Nb layers and disappears upon cooling into the pinned vortex state, indicating that mobile Abrikosov vortices provide an additional channel for spin-angular-momentum dissipation. These results establish ferromagnetic resonance as a sensitive probe of dissipative vortex dynamics in superconductors.

</details>


### [57] [Superconducting Pairing Symmetry on Geometric-Algebra Foundations via the Scalar-Projection Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33806)
*Youping Dai*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper reformulates low-energy superconducting pairing theory algebraically in the Clifford algebra Cl_{3,0}, showing BCS and its extensions (s-, p-, d-wave) map onto an eight-dimensional real multivector space and recover standard results, with three new algebraic theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard complex-matrix formulation of BCS pairing theory obscures the algebraic/structural origin of the singlet-triplet channel dichotomy. This work aims to reconstruct pairing theory in geometric algebra Cl_{3,0} to expose algebraic roots of this dichotomy and to derive new results (geometric decomposition, Fierz sign duality, tomographic error bounds) with applications to chiral superconductors and Weyl semimetals.

Method: A two-level projection: matrix blocks are represented as Clifford multivectors, and grade-0 projected geometric products extract scalar blocks to build Hamiltonians. The fermionic identity B†(V)=-B†(V^T) yields the pairing-channel dichotomy, and Fierz rearrangement is realized by the map Φ:G↦Ge_{31} (e_{31}^2=-1). A kernel/matrix dictionary is established: grades 0⊕3 → spin singlets, grades 1⊕2 → triplets; singlet plane = span{e_2, e_{31}}. BCS limit corresponds to kernel grade 0 (scalar glue) and vertex grade 2 bivector (iσ_2 ↔ e_{31}).

Result: Three algebraic results: (i) a decomposition theorem for BdG quantum geometry with a null-texture criterion, applied to the CsV_3Sb_5 chiral-state controversy; (ii) Fierz sign duality in 4D Minkowski algebra with chirality-reversal extension to two-node Weyl-semimetal models; (iii) an error bound and condition-number structure for tomographic reconstruction under a linear-response probe. Illustrations include a glue-generation mechanism, a one-loop algebraic renormalization flow whose feeding constant matches the su(2) Casimir in magnitude, closed-form μ=0 inter-node spectra, and a UTe_2 two-phase analysis.

Conclusion: Superconducting pairing theory admits a physically equivalent algebraic reconstruction in Cl_{3,0}, where the singlet-triplet dichotomy, Fierz rearrangement, and BCS limiting structure acquire a natural algebraic interpretation. This reformulation yields new analytical tools—BdG quantum-geometry decomposition, Fierz sign duality, and tomographic error bounds—with applications to chiral-state and Weyl-semimetal physics.

Abstract: We formulate the low-energy theory of superconducting pairing in the geometric algebra $Cl_{3,0}$. Conventional BCS mean-field theory and its extensions ($s$-, $p$-, $d$-wave, etc.) have algebraic counterparts in an eight-dimensional real multivector space. The construction is a two-level projection: matrix blocks are represented as Clifford multivectors, and their grade-0 projected geometric products extract the scalar blocks from which Hamiltonians are built. The result is an algebraic reconstruction, physically equivalent to the standard complex-matrix formulation. In this reconstruction, the dichotomy of pairing channels acquires an algebraic root: it follows from the fermionic identity $B^\dagger(V)=-B^\dagger(V^{\mathsf T})$, and the Fierz rearrangement is realized by the map $Φ:G\mapsto Ge_{31}$ (with $e_{31}^2=-1$). This yields a kernel/matrix dictionary: at the effective-kernel level grades $0\oplus3$ correspond to spin singlets and grades $1\oplus2$ to triplets; at the pairing-matrix level the singlet plane is $\mathrm{span}\{e_2,e_{31}\}$. The BCS limit has a double grade identity: kernel grade 0 (scalar glue) and vertex grade 2, a bivector ($iσ_2\leftrightarrow e_{31}$). We state three algebraic results: (i) a decomposition theorem for BdG quantum geometry with a null-texture criterion, applied to the chiral-state controversy in CsV$_3$Sb$_5$; (ii) a Fierz sign duality in the four-dimensional Minkowski algebra, with a chirality-reversal extension to two-node Weyl-semimetal models; (iii) an error bound and condition-number structure for tomographic reconstruction under a linear-response probe model. Illustrations include a glue-generation mechanism, a one-loop algebraic renormalization flow whose feeding constant coincides in magnitude with the $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ Casimir, closed-form $μ=0$ inter-node spectra, and a UTe$_2$ two-phase analysis.

</details>


### [58] [Unconventional superconductivity in locally non-centrosymmetric CeNi$_2$As$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35004)
*Félix Morineau,Jan Knapp,Javier Landaeta,Thomas Lühmann,Lea Richter,Petra Knappova,Soumen Ash,Sushma Lakshmi Ravi Sankar,Konstantin Semeniuk,Elena Hassinger,Christoph Geibel,Manuel Brando,Daniel F. Agterberg,Andrew P. Mackenzie,Seunghyun Khim*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Single crystals of the CaBe2Ge2-type isomorph of CeNi2As2 were grown, realizing heavy fermion superconductivity that condenses from an incoherent normal state quantitatively similar to UBe13.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Intense interest in heavy fermion superconductivity has been driven by the discovery of multiple superconducting phases in UTe2 and CeRh2As2. In CeRh2As2, two field-induced superconducting phases along the c axis are linked to local inversion symmetry breaking. To better understand these phenomena, more examples of heavy fermion superconductivity in locally non-centrosymmetric structures are needed.

Method: The authors grew single crystals of the CaBe2Ge2 isomorph of CeNi2As2, a locally non-centrosymmetric heavy fermion compound.

Result: Superconductivity was observed, allowing comparison with CeRh2As2. Notably, the superconducting condensation emerges from an incoherent normal state quantitatively similar to that of UBe13.

Conclusion: These findings raise profound questions not only about superconductivity in locally non-centrosymmetric structures but about unconventional superconductivity itself.

Abstract: Recent years have seen intense research on heavy fermion superconductivity, inspired by the discovery of multiple superconducting phases in UTe$_\text{2}$ and CeRh$_\text{2}$As$_\text{2}$. In the latter material, two superconducting phases observed under applied magnetic field oriented along the crystallographic $\textit{c}$ direction are associated with local inversion symmetry breaking. To advance understanding of these phenomena, it is highly desirable to find further examples of heavy fermion superconductivity in materials with locally non-centrosymmetric structure. We have succeeded in this quest, by growing single crystals of the CaBe$_\text{2}$Ge$_\text{2}$ isomorph of CeNi$_\text{2}$As$_\text{2}$. The resulting superconductivity brings more than we had foreseen. In addition to providing the opportunity to compare and contrast with that of CeRh$_\text{2}$As$_\text{2}$, the condensation occurs from an incoherent normal state quantitatively similar to that of UBe$_\text{13}$. Our findings therefore raise profound questions not just about superconductivity in the locally non-centrosymmetric structures, but about unconventional superconductivity itself.

</details>


### [59] [Low-Temperature Stabilization of $δ$-NbN Superconducting Thin Films through Energy-Selective Ion Beam Sputtering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35091)
*Yifan Liu,Junwoo Lee,Mingyu Xu,Weiwei Xie,Robert J. Cava,Qi Hua Fan*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Room-temperature magnetron sputtering with an energy-selective ion source enables independent control of ion energy and flux, yielding high-quality NbN thin films with narrowed superconducting transition width at low temperatures, offering a non-equilibrium route for CMOS-compatible quantum device fabrication.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-quality superconducting thin films typically require elevated temperatures, which conflicts with scalable quantum device integration and CMOS-compatible fabrication. Stabilizing high-quality NbN at reduced temperatures has been a longstanding limitation.

Method: A room-temperature magnetron sputtering approach using a novel energy-selective ion source that allows independent control of ion energy and ion flux, enabling precise tailoring of surface kinetics and crystallization pathways. Films were characterized by superconducting transport measurements, XRD, XPS, and TEM.

Result: Compared with conventional reactive sputtering, ion beam-assisted growth produced a substantial narrowing of the superconducting transition width, indicating improved phase purity and electronic homogeneity.

Conclusion: Energy-selective ion control is introduced as a general, non-equilibrium route to stabilize metastable superconducting phases at low temperatures, providing a new design paradigm for thin-film quantum materials.

Abstract: Achieving high-quality superconducting thin films at low temperatures is a central challenge for scalable quantum device integration and Complementary Metal-Oxide-Semiconductor (CMOS)-compatible fabrication. Here, we demonstrate a room-temperature magnetron sputtering approach for synthesizing niobium nitride (NbN) thin films using a novel energy-selective ion source that enables independent control of ion energy and ion flux. This capability provides a powerful route to precisely tailor surface kinetics and crystallization pathways, overcoming longstanding limitations in stabilizing high-quality NbN at reduced temperatures. The resulting films were characterized by superconducting transport measurements, X-ray diffraction, X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy, and transmission electron microscopy. Compared with conventional reactive sputtering, ion beam-assisted growth yields a substantial narrowing of the transition width at the superconducting temperature, indicating improved phase purity and electronic homogeneity. Furthermore, this work introduces energy-selective ion control as a general, non-equilibrium route to stabilize metastable superconducting phases at low temperatures, offering a new design paradigm for thin-film quantum materials.

</details>


### [60] [Improving the critical current density of the $\mathrm{V}_{0.59}\mathrm{Ti}_{0.40}\mathrm{Ce}_{0.01}$ alloy superconductor through successive cold-working and annealing at different temperatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35169)
*Asi Khandelwal,SK. Ramjan,Basudev Padhi,L. S. Sharath Chandra,Archna Sagdeo,Kranti Kumar,Sudip Pal,M. K. Chattopadhyay*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Successive cold working and annealing (SCA) of V0.59Ti0.40Ce0.01 at 550°C yields a gradual rise in Tc and a large Jc enhancement after the first anneal, with weakly field-dependent Jc up to 8.5 T. Grain boundaries pin flux at low fields, while dislocations and β-α' interfaces dominate at high fields. Compared with SCA at 450 and 650°C, 550°C gives the most balanced field dependence and the largest Jc gain relative to the as-cast alloy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The critical current density of V-Ti alloy superconductors is governed by the size and distribution of microstructural defects that pin magnetic flux lines. Since SCA temperature strongly affects defect generation, phase evolution and recovery, the study aims to identify the optimal processing temperature by evaluating SCA at 550°C and comparing it with previously reported SCA at 450 and 650°C.

Method: The V0.59Ti0.40Ce0.01 alloy was subjected to successive cold working and annealing (SCA) at 550°C. Microstructure, superconducting properties (including Tc and Jc over a wide magnetic-field range) and flux-pinning behaviour were characterized, and pinning force density analysis was used to identify the dominant pinning centres. Results were compared with SCA at 450 and 650°C.

Result: During SCA at 550°C, Tc increases gradually with successive processing steps. The first anneal after 50% cold rolling produces a significant Jc enhancement across the measured field range, whereas later SCA cycles give only marginal changes. Jc remains weakly field-dependent over a wide field range, and the final cold-worked sample retains a finite Jc up to 8.5 T. Pinning force density analysis shows grain boundaries dominate at low fields, while dislocations and β-α' interfaces become dominant at higher fields. Among processing temperatures, 650°C gives the highest low-field Jc, 450°C the best high-field performance, while 550°C yields the most balanced field dependence and the largest Jc enhancement relative to the as-cast alloy. The highest absolute high-field Jc occurs after the third SCA cycle at 450°C, but a comparable value is reached after only the first anneal at 550°C.

Conclusion: The intermediate annealing temperature of 550°C provides an effective balance between defect generation, phase evolution and recovery, resulting in enhanced flux pinning over a wide magnetic-field range and offering a more processing-efficient route to high Jc.

Abstract: The critical current density ($J_c$) of V-Ti alloy superconductors is strongly influenced by the size and distribution of microstructural defects that pin magnetic flux lines. In this work, the effect of successive cold working and annealing (SCA) at 550°C on the microstructure, superconducting properties, and flux-pinning behaviour of the V$_{0.59}$Ti$_{0.40}$Ce$_{0.01}$ alloy is investigated and compared with the previously reported SCA at 450 and 650°C. During SCA at 550°C, the superconducting transition temperature increases gradually with successive processing steps. The first annealing after cold rolling to 50% thickness produces a significant enhancement in $J_c$ over the measured field range, whereas subsequent SCA cycles result in only marginal changes. The $J_c$ remains relatively weakly dependent on magnetic field over wide magnetic-field range, and the final cold-worked sample exhibits a finite $J_c$ up to 8.5 T. Pinning force density analysis shows that grain boundaries dominate flux pinning in low magnetic fields, whereas dislocations and $β$-$α'$ interfaces become the dominant pinning centres in higher fields. Comparison of the different SCA temperatures shows that 650°C provides the highest low-field $J_c$, whereas 450°C gives the best high-field performance. In contrast, SCA at 550°C provides most balanced field dependence and the largest enhancement in $J_c$ relative to the corresponding as-cast alloy. Although the highest absolute $J_c$ in the high-field regime is achieved after the third SCA cycle at 450°C, a comparable value is obtained after only the first annealing at 550°C. These results demonstrate that the intermediate annealing temperature of 550°C provides an effective balance between defect generation, phase evolution, and recovery, resulting in enhanced flux pinning over a wide magnetic-field range.

</details>


### [61] [Periodic Trends and a Physics-Based Multistage Search for High-$T_c$ Hydride Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35582)
*Tianran Chen,Taner Yildirim*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The authors develop an interpretable multistage workflow using the spectral moment A1 (approximated by αβρ) to screen over 100,000 binary hydride structures for conventional superconductivity, prioritizing NaH6 families, with Pm-3m NaH6 predicted to have Tc ≈ 230 K.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: First-principles electron–phonon-coupling (EPC) calculations for hydrogen-rich superconductors under pressure are computationally expensive, so there is a need for cheap, interpretable descriptors to prioritize candidate binary hydrides before full EPC calculations.

Method: Controlled cubic XmHn prototypes at 200 GPa are used to define the spectral moment A1 as a measure of electron–phonon perturbation strength, constructed to remain finite as harmonic phonons soften. A1 is correlated with the H-1s Fermi-level DOS fraction α, a projected Fermi-surface descriptor β, and atomic number density ρ, summarized heuristically as A1 ≈ hαβρ. These descriptors plus enthalpic competitiveness feed a three-stage evolutionary search: multiobjective structural screening, harmonic-phonon stability filtering, and coarse DFPT EPC calculations.

Result: Applied to over 100,000 binary hydride structures at 50 and 200 GPa, the workflow identifies candidates and prioritizes NaH6 structural families. Higher-accuracy calculations show Pm-3m NaH6 is dynamically stable down to 50 GPa with a harmonic Allen–Dynes Tc ≈ 230 K.

Conclusion: The framework combines electronic descriptors with progressively more expensive stability and EPC calculations, enabling scalable hydride exploration. The descriptor is intended for ranking, not as a universal Tc predictor, and does not establish superconductivity or physical realizability for unstable structures.

Abstract: Hydrogen-rich materials under pressure are candidates for conventional phonon-mediated superconductivity, but first-principles electron--phonon-coupling (EPC) calculations are computationally demanding. We develop an interpretable multistage workflow for prioritizing binary hydride superconductors before full EPC calculations. Using controlled cubic $X_m$H$_n$ prototypes at 200~GPa, we introduce the spectral moment $A_1$ as an intermediate measure of electron--phonon perturbation strength. In its linewidth-based construction, $A_1$ remains finite as harmonic phonons soften, allowing related structures with imaginary harmonic modes to be screened; it does not establish superconductivity or physical realizability for unstable structures. For dynamically stable cubic systems, $A_1$ correlates strongly with the H-$1s$ density-of-states fraction at the Fermi level, $α$, a projected Fermi-surface descriptor, $β$, and atomic number density, $ρ$. These trends are summarized heuristically by $A_1\approx hαβρ$, where $h$ is framework- and pressure-dependent; the descriptor is used for ranking, not as a universal $T_c$ predictor.
  We incorporate $αβρ$ and enthalpic competitiveness into a three-stage evolutionary search comprising multiobjective structural screening, harmonic-phonon stability filtering, and coarse density-functional perturbation-theory (DFPT) EPC calculations. Application to more than 100,000 binary hydride structures at 50 and 200~GPa identifies candidates and prioritizes NaH$_6$ structural families. Higher-accuracy calculations show that $Pm\bar{3}m$ NaH$_6$ is dynamically stable down to 50~GPa and has a harmonic Allen--Dynes estimate of $T_c\approx230$~K. The framework combines electronic descriptors with progressively more expensive stability and EPC calculations for scalable hydride exploration.

</details>

### [62] [R-transforms for non-Hermitian block random matrices: a spherical integral approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31730)
*Pierre Bousseyroux*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the spherical-integral approach to R-transforms of non-Hermitian random matrices to matrices with a fixed K×K block structure, introducing rank-K spherical integrals and matrix-valued R-transforms, and derives a conjectural subordination relation for sums of independent block matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing spherical-integral methods for R-transforms of non-Hermitian random matrices do not cover matrices with a fixed block structure, motivating an extension to handle joint block structure and determine spectral boundaries.

Method: The authors extend the spherical-integral approach to fixed K×K block-structured non-Hermitian random matrices. They introduce a rank-K spherical integral defining a scalar H-transform on 2K×2K overlap matrices, together with an associated matrix-valued R-transform encoding the joint block structure. The replica method is used to derive a conjectural subordination relation for sums of independent block matrices.

Result: A rank-K spherical integral and scalar H-transform on 2K×2K overlap matrices are defined; a matrix-valued R-transform encoding the joint block structure is constructed; and a conjectural subordination relation for sums of independent block matrices is derived via the replica method.

Conclusion: The proposed framework offers a method for determining the spectral boundaries of large non-Hermitian random matrices with a fixed block structure.

Abstract: We extend the spherical-integral approach to $\mathcal{R}$-transforms of non-Hermitian random matrices to random matrices with a fixed $K \times K$ block structure. We introduce a rank-$K$ spherical integral defining a scalar $H$-transform on $2K \times 2K$ overlap matrices and an associated matrix-valued $\mathcal{R}$-transform encoding the joint block structure. Using the replica method, we derive a conjectural subordination relation for sums of independent block matrices. This framework provides a method for determining the spectral boundaries of large non-Hermitian random matrices with a fixed block structure.

</details>


### [63] [Connecting finite-size scaling and renormalization-group flows in Anderson localization on random graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31872)
*Ignacio García-Mata,Weitao Chen,John Martin,Bertrand Georgeot,Gabriel Lemarié*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: The paper links finite-size scaling of eigenfunction moments with RG flows of multifractal dimensions D_q for the Anderson transition on small-world graphs, showing that the observed family of RG trajectories for D_1 does not require two-parameter scaling or Kosterlitz-Thouless behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Two complementary descriptions of the Anderson transition on small-world random graphs exist—finite-size scaling of eigenfunction moments and RG flows of multifractal dimensions D_q—and it was previously suggested that the family of RG trajectories for the information dimension D_1 might imply two-parameter scaling and Kosterlitz-Thouless-like critical behavior, which the authors aim to clarify.

Method: The authors compute renormalization-group flows of multifractal dimensions D_q, extending earlier results for the information dimension D_1 to other moment orders, including small q<1/2. They construct a beta function describing the flow of eigenfunction moments and test its collapse onto a single function across different disorder strengths to probe single-parameter scaling.

Result: The RG flows recover the qualitative structure previously reported for D_1, and extension to q<1/2 confirms the strongly multifractal nature of the localized phase with a clear separation between multifractal and localized behaviors at strong disorder. The constructed beta function for eigenfunction moments collapses onto a single function across disorder strengths, confirming single-parameter scaling. Large q>1/2 and small q<1/2 moments exhibit distinct critical behaviors with different critical exponents.

Conclusion: The family of RG trajectories observed for D_1 does not necessarily imply two-parameter scaling or Kosterlitz-Thouless-like critical behavior. Characterizing these properties requires considering distinct observables, namely large q>1/2 and small q<1/2 eigenfunction moments, which are associated with different critical behaviors and critical exponents.

Abstract: We investigate the relation between two complementary descriptions of the Anderson transition on small-world random graphs: finite-size scaling of eigenfunction moments and renormalization-group (RG) flows of multifractal dimensions $D_q$. The RG flows recover the qualitative structure previously reported for the information dimension $D_1$, while their extension to other $D_q$ with smaller moment orders $q<1/2$ confirms the strongly multifractal nature of the localized phase characterized by a clear separation between multifractal and localized behaviors at strong disorder. On the other hand, we show that we can construct another beta function characterizing the flow of eigenfunction moments, whose collapse onto a single function across different disorder strengths provides a clear confirmation of single-parameter scaling property. These results indicate that the family of RG trajectories observed for the information dimension $D_1$ need not imply two-parameter scaling and Kosterlitz-Thouless like critical behavior. To characterize these properties, one should consider different observables, such as large $q>1/2$ and small $q<1/2$ eigenfunction moments, which are associated with distinct critical behaviors, in particular different critical exponents.

</details>


### [64] [Dense auto-hetero associative memories applied to noisy communication channels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32605)
*Elena Agliari,Andrea Alessandrelli,Adriano Barra,Alberto Fachechi*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: The paper introduces a dense, higher-order extension of modular Hebbian networks for pattern disentanglement, enabling a storage capacity that scales linearly with module size, supported by statistical-mechanical analysis and two decoding applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior modular Hebbian networks achieved pattern disentanglement (retrieving individual stored patterns from their spurious mixture) only with pairwise interactions, which severely limits the number of patterns that can be handled.

Method: The authors promote both intra-module auto-associative couplings and inter-module hetero-associative couplings to higher-order Hebbian interactions, selecting suitable interaction orders. They perform a statistical-mechanical analysis using Guerra's interpolation to derive self-consistency equations for the order parameters and construct phase diagrams, validated by Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: With appropriate interaction orders, the network disentangles mixtures while storing a number of patterns that scales linearly with module size—a regime where the pairwise counterpart fails. Monte Carlo simulations confirm the theoretical phase diagrams identifying the disentanglement region.

Conclusion: Disentanglement serves as a natural decoding primitive, demonstrated via explicit reconstruction of hidden patterns from Hebbian tensors and unlabeled mixtures, and a communication protocol transmitting tokens as masked mixtures. Its attractor-based decoding degrades gracefully under strong channel corruption, unlike conventional secure-transmission pipelines.

Abstract: Networks of interacting Hebbian networks have recently been shown to perform a task beyond associative memory, namely \emph{pattern disentanglement}: when fed with a spurious mixture of stored patterns, the different modules spontaneously specialize on, and retrieve, the different constituents of the mixture. So far, this capability has only been established for pairwise interactions, which limits the number of patterns that can be handled. Here we introduce a dense extension of these modular networks, in which both the auto-associative couplings within each module and the hetero-associative couplings among modules are promoted to higher-order Hebbian interactions. We show that, with a suitable choice of the interaction orders, the network disentangles mixtures while storing a number of patterns that scales linearly with the module size, a regime where its pairwise counterpart fails. Through a statistical-mechanical analysis based on Guerra's interpolation, we derive the self-consistency equations for the order parameters and draw the phase diagrams identifying the region where disentanglement is achieved; these predictions are confirmed by Monte Carlo simulations. Finally, we show that disentanglement provides a natural decoding primitive, and we illustrate it with two applications: the explicit reconstruction of all the hidden patterns from the Hebbian tensors and a stream of unlabeled mixtures, and a proof-of-concept communication protocol in which each message token is transmitted as a masked mixture of hidden patterns and decoded by the network dynamics. Owing to its attractor-based decoding, the protocol degrades gracefully under strong channel corruption, where conventional secure-transmission pipelines fail abruptly.

</details>


### [65] [Trimers, pairs and localisation on disordered random graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32768)
*Alexei Vazquez*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: Emergent hyperedges arise as k-body bound states (e.g., Borromean trimers) from simple pairwise dynamics on sparse random graphs, and their Anderson localisation transition is dramatically more fragile to disorder than that of single particles or pairs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Higher-order networks usually treat group interactions as primitive. This paper asks whether group interactions can instead emerge from underlying pairwise dynamics on a graph, and when such emergent hyperedges become relevant at large scale—specifically at the Anderson localisation transition that determines whether they matter macroscopically.

Method: The work models an emergent hyperedge as a k-body bound state of a Hamiltonian with terms on graph vertices and edges, and defines a Borromean hyperedge as one whose (k-1)-body faces are all unbound. It detects these on finite graphs via a k-fold coincidence that survives the thermodynamic limit while (k-1)-fold coincidences vanish. Thresholds are computed on the Bethe lattice in closed form for the pair, in both the condensate and Fermi-liquid sectors. The Anderson transition of the composite is analysed via the single-particle cavity transition using the composite's hopping and effective disorder.

Result: Sparse random graphs generate emergent hyperedges from the simplest pairwise dynamics: three distinguishable particles with on-site attraction U and hopping t bind into a trimer before any pair binds (Borromean). On disordered graphs, emergent hyperedges nucleate on rare deep sites and localise before their constituents. The compact trimer (narrow band, threefold sensitivity to site energies) undergoes the Anderson transition at disorder ~1.6t, versus ~17t for a single particle and ~4t for a pair. The Fermi liquid of trimers becomes a glass above a line between 1.5t and 1.7t, whereas a liquid of single particles at the same density needs 15t–16t.

Conclusion: A group interaction made by pairwise dynamics is about an order of magnitude more fragile to heterogeneity than the network that makes it. The composite's transition is effectively the single-particle cavity transition with the composite's hopping and effective disorder.

Abstract: Higher-order networks take group interactions as given. I ask what happens when a group interaction is made by pairwise dynamics on a graph, and follow it through the transition that decides whether it matters at large scale, Anderson localisation. An emergent hyperedge is a $k$-body bound state of a Hamiltonian whose terms live on the vertices and edges of the graph, Borromean when none of its $(k-1)$-body faces is bound, detected on a finite graph by a $k$-fold coincidence that survives the thermodynamic limit while the $(k-1)$-fold ones vanish. Sparse random graphs produce one from the simplest pairwise dynamics: three distinguishable particles with on-site attraction $U$ and hopping $t$ bind into a trimer before any pair binds. The thresholds are obtained on the Bethe lattice, in closed form for the pair, in both sectors that an expander graph has and a lattice lacks, the one a condensate sees and the one a Fermi liquid sees. On a disordered graph the emergent hyperedges nucleate on rare deep sites and localise before their parts: the compact trimer, with a narrow band and a threefold sensitivity to site energies, undergoes the Anderson transition at a disorder of $1.6t$, against $17t$ for a single particle and $4t$ for a pair, and the Fermi liquid of trimers is a glass above a line between $1.5t$ and $1.7t$, where a liquid of single particles at the same density needs $15t$ to $16t$. The transition is the single-particle cavity transition with the composite's hopping and effective disorder. A group interaction made by pairwise dynamics is an order of magnitude more fragile to heterogeneity than the network that makes it.

</details>


### [66] [Improved Learning of Molecular Energetics Through an Electron-Wise Joint Charge Density and Energy Objective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32858)
*Vadim Ionas,Jonas Elsborg,Felix Ærtebjerg,Arghya Bhowmik*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: A graph neural network jointly predicts GGA electron densities and B3LYP-level molecular energies via a shared equivariant representation, showing that electron density information improves and accelerates accurate energy learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate energies require expensive hybrid functional calculations such as B3LYP, while cheaper GGA-level electron densities contain rich spatial information. The authors aim to leverage this low-cost density information to improve and accelerate the learning of more accurate energies, and to unify charge density and energy prediction in a single practical framework for atomic-scale simulations.

Method: A graph neural network-based learning framework is trained jointly with two prediction heads: one for electron densities calculated with a GGA functional and one for molecular energies obtained from hybrid functional (B3LYP) calculations. An equivariant molecular representation is shared across both density and energy prediction heads.

Result: Joint training succeeds, indicating that the rich spatial information in the electron density distribution can be used to improve and accelerate the learning of accurate energies, while the shared equivariant representation lets the model learn complementary molecular quantities within a unified framework.

Conclusion: Sharing an equivariant representation across density and energy heads enables complementary learning and suggests a promising route for practical use of recent charge density learning frameworks in atomic-scale simulations.

Abstract: We present a graph neural network-based learning framework trained to jointly predict electron densities and molecular energies. The model is trained to predict electron densities calculated with a Generalized Gradient Approximation (GGA) functional while simultaneously learning to predict molecular energies obtained with hybrid functional calculations at the B3LYP level of theory. Our results indicate that the rich spatial information in electron density distribution can be used to improve and accelerate the learning of accurate energies. By sharing an equivariant molecular representation across density and energy prediction heads, the model learns complementary molecular quantities within a unified framework. This provides a new promising route for practical use cases of many recent charge density learning frameworks for atomic scale simulations.

</details>


### [67] [Pre-registered tests of solid-state-physics-inspired LLM compression: a cluster-level negative result at small-language-model scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34292)
*Jun-qiang Lu*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: A three-month pre-registered research-agent program tested five solid-state-physics-inspired compression hypotheses on pretrained language models using a 3-sigma gate; nearly all predictions were falsified (17 of 18 catalogue studies negative), and the pre-registered expectation that attention heads behave like Kohn-nearsighted insulators inverted at the tested scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper probes whether physics-inspired mappings (area-law / Kohn-nearsighted decay of the one-particle density matrix; density-matrix and tensor-network rank structure) transfer usefully to compressing or characterizing pretrained language-model representations. It aims to test these mappings rigorously with committed-before-data predictions, rather than reporting post-hoc fits.

Method: Five mappings were tested across two faces: a distance face (P001 Wannier, P002 tight-binding) and a rank face (P003 DMRG-truncated MLPs, P005 Wilson-RG, P011 tensor-train embeddings). Predictions were committed to git before pilot data, with a 3-sigma gate deciding PASS or SHELVE and a pre-registration + cluster-framing + append-only-catalogue discipline. Tests used GPT-2-medium and Pythia-160M attention-versus-distance fits, sparsity baselines (PCA, random-Haar, identity), per-token tensor-train bond dimension versus surprisal, and cross-paper correlations; the rank-face no-gain result was extended to 7-8B models.

Result: P005 was pre-empted at Phase 1 and three of four Phase-3 pilots were falsified. GPT-2-medium attention-versus-distance was best fit by a stretched exponential in 12 of 16 median-layer heads once probe padding was excluded; a tight-binding cutoff cost +96% perplexity (P002). On Pythia-160M the Wannier sparsity 0.054 +/- 0.004 was indistinguishable from PCA, random-Haar and identity baselines (P001). Per-token tensor-train bond dimension did not track surprisal (r = 0.016 vs pre-registered 0.65) and inflated rather than compressed (P011). P003 was mixed: scaling claim shelved (r = -0.434), MPO premise died at stage-0, and its cross-paper check collapsed from r = 0.523 to 0.047 under the same correction, leaving both cross-paper checks null. The catalogue holds eighteen concluded studies, seventeen negative.

Conclusion: The results invert the pre-registered prediction that most attention heads behave like Kohn-nearsighted insulators, pointing instead to critical, glassy or heavy-tailed regimes; the inversion is specific to the <= 350M scale while the rank-face no-gain result held to 7-8B. Contributions are the pre-registration + 3-sigma + cluster-framing + append-only-catalogue discipline (including why the enforcement gate was designed but not deployed), four pre-registered negative results with full data release, and the inversion.

Abstract: We report a three-month autonomous research-agent program testing five solid-state-physics-inspired compression mappings on pretrained language models, with predictions committed to git before any pilot data and a 3-sigma gate deciding PASS or SHELVE. The common anchor -- area-law / Kohn-nearsighted decay of the one-particle density matrix -- has a distance face (P001 Wannier, P002 tight-binding) and a rank face (P003 DMRG-truncated MLPs, P005 Wilson-RG, P011 tensor-train embeddings). P005 was pre-empted at Phase 1; three of four Phase-3 pilots were falsified. On the attention face, GPT-2-medium attention-versus-distance is best fit by a stretched exponential in 12 of 16 median-layer heads once probe padding is excluded, and a tight-binding cutoff costs +96% perplexity (P002); on Pythia-160M the Wannier sparsity 0.054 +/- 0.004 is indistinguishable from PCA, random-Haar and identity baselines (P001). On the rank face, per-token tensor-train bond dimension does not track surprisal (r = 0.016 vs a pre-registered 0.65) and the format inflates rather than compresses (P011). P003 is mixed: its scaling claim shelved (r = -0.434), its MPO premise died at stage-0, and its cross-paper check, r = 0.523 as first written, collapses to 0.047 under the same correction, leaving both cross-paper checks null. The results invert the pre-registered prediction that most attention heads behave like Kohn-nearsighted insulators, pointing instead to critical, glassy or heavy-tailed regimes; the inversion is specific to the <= 350M scale tested, while the rank-face no-gain result held to 7-8B. We contribute the pre-registration + 3-sigma + cluster-framing + append-only-catalogue discipline -- including why our own enforcement gate was designed but not deployed -- four pre-registered negative results with full data release, and the inversion. The catalogue holds eighteen concluded studies, seventeen negative.

</details>


### [68] [Localization in tight-binding models with power-law distributed couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34767)
*Maximilian Weigmann,Luca Schaefer,Barbara Drossel*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: Localization in 1D and 2D tight-binding models with power-law distributed couplings is compared between Laplacian and adjacency matrices. Adjacency eigenmodes localize at large sizes, while the Laplacian retains a small fraction of system-spanning modes due to a conservation law, producing power-law tails in the participation ratio distribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Laplacian and adjacency matrices govern distinct dynamical processes, yet their localization properties under power-law distributed couplings are not systematically compared, motivating a unified spectral and localization study.

Method: Numerical diagonalization of 1D and 2D tight-binding Laplacian and adjacency matrices with power-law distributed couplings, across varying system sizes and connectivities, comparing spectra and eigenmode localization via participation ratio.

Result: Adjacency eigenmodes are all localized for sufficiently large systems; the Laplacian yields a small proportion of system-spanning modes due to a conservation law, giving power-law tails in the participation ratio distribution and its eigenvalue relation. In 1D the exponent varies continuously with the coupling-distribution exponent; in 2D the largest-relaxation-time modes shift from system-spanning to localized when the coupling exponent exceeds 0.75.

Conclusion: The conservation law inherent to the Laplacian qualitatively distinguishes its localization behavior from the adjacency matrix; phenomenological explanations account for the observed power laws and the 2D localization transition at exponent 0.75.

Abstract: We study the localization properties of 1D and 2D tight-binding models with power-law distributed couplings by comparing the spectrum and the localization properties of the eigenmodes of the Laplacian and the adjacency matrix, using numerical diagonalization of these matrices for different system sizes and connectivities. These two matrices are relevant for different types of dynamical processes. While all eigenmodes of the adjacency matrix are localized for sufficiently large system sizes, the Laplacian matrix always leads to a small proportion of system-spanning modes due to a conservation law, and therefore to power-law tails in the probability distribution of the participation ratio and its relation to the eigenvalues. In one dimension, the exponent of these power laws change continuously with the exponent that characterizes the distribution of couplings. In two dimensions, the modes with the largest relaxation times change from system-spanning to localized when the exponent of the distribution of couplings becomes larger than 0.75. We provide phenomenological explanations for all these findings.

</details>


### [69] [Exact probability distributions of complex spacing ratios in non-Hermitian random matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35230)
*Kohei Kawabata*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: The paper derives finite-N complex spacing ratio distributions for non-Hermitian Gaussian random matrix ensembles in symmetry classes AI† (complex symmetric) and AII† (complex self-dual), starting from exact joint eigenvalue probability densities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The complex spacing ratio is a useful diagnostic of spectral correlations and nonintegrability in open quantum systems, but finite-N distributions had not been derived from exact joint eigenvalue probability densities for these non-Hermitian symmetry classes.

Method: Derive finite-N complex-spacing-ratio distributions from exact joint eigenvalue probability densities for the Gaussian ensembles of non-Hermitian random matrices in classes AI† and AII†. In class AII†, obtain an exact algebraic expression for arbitrary N and evaluate distributions and moments for N=3,4,5,6. In class AI†, reduce the joint density (which retains a noncompact integral over nonunitary eigenvector degrees of freedom) to a normalized one-dimensional integral representation for N=3 and derive asymptotic behavior. Confirm results by direct numerical diagonalization.

Result: Exact algebraic expression for the complex spacing ratio distribution at arbitrary N in class AII†, with explicit distributions and representative moments for N=3,4,5,6. For class AI†, a normalized one-dimensional integral representation at N=3, plus asymptotic results including a logarithmic correction to the cubic level repulsion and a nonanalytic contribution to the angular density. Numerical diagonalization confirms the analytical results.

Conclusion: Exact finite-N complex spacing ratio statistics are obtained for non-Hermitian Gaussian ensembles in classes AI† and AII†, revealing characteristic level-repulsion and angular-density features and validating prior universality expectations.

Abstract: The complex spacing ratio is the complex displacement from a reference eigenvalue to its nearest neighbor divided by the corresponding displacement to its next-to-nearest neighbor. Its statistics provide a useful diagnostic of spectral correlations and nonintegrability in open quantum systems. Here, starting from the exact joint eigenvalue probability densities, we derive finite-$N$ complex-spacing-ratio distributions for the Gaussian ensembles of non-Hermitian random matrices in classes AI$^†$ and AII$^†$, realized by complex symmetric and complex self-dual random matrices, respectively. In class AII$^†$, we obtain an exact algebraic expression for arbitrary $N$ and explicitly evaluate the distributions and representative moments for $N=3, 4, 5, 6$. In class AI$^†$, although the joint density retains a noncompact integral over nonunitary eigenvector degrees of freedom, we analytically derive a normalized one-dimensional integral representation for $N=3$ and determine the asymptotic behavior, including a logarithmic correction to the cubic level repulsion and a nonanalytic contribution to the angular density. We further confirm these analytical results through direct numerical diagonalization of non-Hermitian random matrices.

</details>


### [70] [Attraction-controlled torque organization and rotational states in frictional granular matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35270)
*Kiwamu Yoshii*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: Numerical study of attractive frictional granular material showing that interparticle attraction strongly modifies stress transmission, torque organization, and particle rotation, thereby controlling macroscopic rheology via reorganization of the frictional contact network.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Interparticle attraction is a central force that exerts no direct torque on particles, yet its role in stress transmission, torque organization, and particle rotation in frictional granular materials remains unclear. The study aims to clarify how attraction, despite lacking direct torque, reshapes the contact network and rheology.

Method: Two-dimensional frictional granular material is numerically simulated. Stress transmission, torque organization, and particle rotation are analyzed as functions of interparticle attraction, packing fraction, and shear rate. Shear stress is decomposed into attractive and repulsive contributions, and the coordination number and local torque-sign correlation are examined.

Result: Attraction produces a low-rate stress state and selects the high-stress state near the shear-thickening regime of the dry system, while leaving the high-rate thickened state almost unchanged. Decomposition shows the direct attractive contribution accounts for only a fraction of total stress, whereas the repulsive contribution is strongly enhanced. Attraction increases coordination number and torque amplitude and can reverse the local torque-sign correlation. At low packing fractions and intermediate shear rates, a stress-collapse state emerges with strongly suppressed torque and rotation despite a finite contact network.

Conclusion: Interparticle attraction controls macroscopic rheology through reorganization of the frictional contact network. Its influence on stress, torque, and rotation is largely indirect, mediated by contact-network restructuring rather than direct torque transmission.

Abstract: In this study, we numerically investigate how interparticle attraction affects stress transmission, torque organization, and particle rotation in a two-dimensional frictional granular material. Although the attraction is a central force and exerts no direct torque on the particles, it strongly modifies the contact network and particle rotation. Attraction produces a low-rate stress state and selects the high-stress state near the shear-thickening regime of the dry system, while leaving the high-rate thickened state almost unchanged. By decomposing the shear stress, we find that the direct attractive contribution accounts for only a fraction of the total stress, while the repulsive contribution is strongly enhanced. Attraction increases the coordination number and torque amplitude and can reverse the local torque-sign correlation. At low packing fractions and intermediate shear rates, a stress-collapse state emerges with strongly suppressed torque and rotation despite a finite contact network. These results show that attraction controls macroscopic rheology through reorganization of the frictional contact network.

</details>
